\doxysubsection{Detailed Description}
Allow the creation of windows, display images on them and manage user events (keyboard, mouse and windows events). 

\doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} methods rely on a low-\/level graphic library to perform\+: it can be either {\bfseries{X-\/\+Window}} (X11, for Unix-\/based systems) or {\bfseries{GDI32}} (for Windows-\/based systems). If both libraries are missing, \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} will not be able to display images on screen, and will enter a minimal mode where warning messages will be outputed each time the program is trying to call one of the \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} method.

The configuration variable {\ttfamily cimg\+\_\+display} tells about the graphic library used. It is set automatically by \textbackslash{}\+CImg when one of these graphic libraries has been detected. But, you can override its value if necessary. Valid choices are\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item 0\+: Disable display capabilities.
\item 1\+: Use {\bfseries{X-\/\+Window}} (X11) library.
\item 2\+: Use {\bfseries{GDI32}} library.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Remember to link your program against {\bfseries{X11}} or {\bfseries{GDI32}} libraries if you use \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display}.

Constructing an empty \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} instance does not make a window appearing on the screen, until display of valid data is performed. 
\end{DoxyNote}

Return {\ttfamily true} if the {\ttfamily ESC} key is being pressed on the associated window, {\ttfamily false} otherwise. 

\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}
Similar methods exist for all keys managed by \textbackslash{}\+CImg (see cimg\+::key\+ESC). 
\end{DoxyNote}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{button()}{button()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a78f937ad8000675d0293a8d7e20627fa} 
unsigned int cimg\+\_\+library\+\_\+suffixed\+::\+CImg\+Display\+::button (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



Return current state of the mouse buttons. 

\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}
Three mouse buttons can be managed. If one button is pressed, its corresponding bit in the returned value is set\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item bit {\ttfamily 0} (value {\ttfamily 0x1})\+: State of the left mouse button.
\item bit {\ttfamily 1} (value {\ttfamily 0x2})\+: State of the right mouse button.
\item bit {\ttfamily 2} (value {\ttfamily 0x4})\+: State of the middle mouse button.
\end{DoxyItemize}
\end{DoxyNote}
Several bits can be activated if more than one button are pressed at the same time. \begin{DoxyParagraph}{Example}

\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a7716ca9a49ff94123b3417556bf4ce49}{CImgDisplay}}\ disp(400,400);}
\DoxyCodeLine{\textcolor{keywordflow}{while}\ (!disp.is\_closed())\ \{}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{keywordflow}{if}\ (disp.button()\&1)\ \{\ \textcolor{comment}{//\ Left\ button\ clicked.}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ ...}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{keywordflow}{if}\ (disp.button()\&2)\ \{\ \textcolor{comment}{//\ Right\ button\ clicked.}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ ...}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{keywordflow}{if}\ (disp.button()\&4)\ \{\ \textcolor{comment}{//\ Middle\ button\ clicked.}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ ...}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ disp.wait();}
\DoxyCodeLine{\}}

\end{DoxyCode}
 
\end{DoxyParagraph}

\mbox{\hyperlink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display}} \& cimg\+\_\+library\+\_\+suffixed\+::\+CImg\+Display\+::close (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



Close (visible) associated window and make it disappear from the screen. 

\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}

\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item A closed display only means the associated window is not visible anymore. This does not mean the display has been destroyed. Use show() to make the associated window reappear.
\item Using close() on a closed display does nothing. 
\end{DoxyItemize}
\end{DoxyNote}

Return a reference to an empty display. 

\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}
Can be useful for writing function prototypes where one of the argument (of type \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display}\&) must have a default value. 
\end{DoxyNote}
\begin{DoxyParagraph}{Example}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{key()}{key()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a8eb7cd4bd6e6593689f82d157caf3ec1} 
unsigned int cimg\+\_\+library\+\_\+suffixed\+::\+CImg\+Display\+::key (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int}]{pos}{ = {\ttfamily 0}}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



Return one entry from the pressed keys history. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em pos} & Indice to read from the pressed keys history (indice {\ttfamily 0} corresponds to latest entry). \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
Keycode of a pressed key or {\ttfamily 0} for a released key. 
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}

\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Each \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} stores a history of the pressed keys in a buffer of size {\ttfamily 128}. When a new key is pressed, its keycode is stored in the pressed keys history. When a key is released, {\ttfamily 0} is put instead. This means that up to the 64 last pressed keys may be read from the pressed keys history. When a new value is stored, the pressed keys history is shifted so that the latest entry is always stored at position {\ttfamily 0}.
\item Keycode constants are defined in the cimg namespace and are architecture-\/dependent. Use them to ensure your code stay portable (see cimg\+::key\+ESC). 
\end{DoxyItemize}
\end{DoxyNote}

\index{normalization@{normalization}!cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay@{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{normalization()}{normalization()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a79b6f929fe4aaf411051fc85862191a7} 
unsigned int cimg\+\_\+library\+\_\+suffixed\+::\+CImg\+Display\+::normalization (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



Return normalization type of the display. 

The normalization type tells about how the values of an input image are normalized by the \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} to be correctly displayed. The range of values for pixels displayed on screen is {\ttfamily \mbox{[}0,255\mbox{]}}. If the range of values of the data to display is different, a normalization may be required for displaying the data in a correct way. The normalization type can be one of\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item {\ttfamily 0}\+: Value normalization is disabled. It is then assumed that all input data to be displayed by the \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} instance have values in range {\ttfamily \mbox{[}0,255\mbox{]}}.
\item {\ttfamily 1}\+: Value normalization is always performed (this is the default behavior). Before displaying an input image, its values will be (virtually) stretched in range {\ttfamily \mbox{[}0,255\mbox{]}}, so that the contrast of the displayed pixels will be maximum. Use this mode for images whose minimum and maximum values are not prescribed to known values (e.\+g. float-\/valued images). Note that when normalized versions of images are computed for display purposes, the actual values of these images are not modified.
\item {\ttfamily 2}\+: Value normalization is performed once (on the first image display), then the same normalization coefficients are kept for next displayed frames.
\item {\ttfamily 3}\+: Value normalization depends on the pixel type of the data to display. For integer pixel types, the normalization is done regarding the minimum/maximum values of the type (no normalization occurs then for {\ttfamily unsigned char}). For float-\/valued pixel types, the normalization is done regarding the minimum/maximum value of the image data instead. 
\end{DoxyItemize}\Hypertarget{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a61454bcb46ac5620760e9488b5819ecd}\index{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay@{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay}!operator bool@{operator bool}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a537d30bd26b82bc6ed4afc8777adb097} 
unsigned int cimg\+\_\+library\+\_\+suffixed\+::\+CImg\+Display\+::released\+\_\+key (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int}]{pos}{ = {\ttfamily 0}}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



Return one entry from the released keys history. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em pos} & Indice to read from the released keys history (indice {\ttfamily 0} corresponds to latest entry). \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
Keycode of a released key or {\ttfamily 0} for a pressed key. 
\end{DoxyReturn}
\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}

\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item Each \doxylink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display} stores a history of the released keys in a buffer of size {\ttfamily 128}. When a new key is released, its keycode is stored in the pressed keys history. When a key is pressed, {\ttfamily 0} is put instead. This means that up to the 64 last released keys may be read from the released keys history. When a new value is stored, the released keys history is shifted so that the latest entry is always stored at position {\ttfamily 0}.
\item Keycode constants are defined in the cimg namespace and are architecture-\/dependent. Use them to ensure your code stay portable (see cimg\+::key\+ESC). 
\end{DoxyItemize}
\end{DoxyNote}
\Hypertarget{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a6f7103d3efe2fcbd7be9535f0139c75b}\index{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay@{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay}!render@{render}}

\mbox{\hyperlink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay}{CImg\+Display}} \& cimg\+\_\+library\+\_\+suffixed\+::\+CImg\+Display\+::set\+\_\+title (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const char \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}const}]{format}{, }\item[{}]{...}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



Set title of the associated window. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em format} & C-\/string containing the format of the title, as with {\ttfamily std\+::printf()}. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyWarning}{Warning}
As the first argument is a format string, it is highly recommended to write 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{disp.set\_title(\textcolor{stringliteral}{"{}\%s"{}},window\_title);}

\end{DoxyCode}
 instead of 
\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{disp.set\_title(window\_title);}

\end{DoxyCode}
 if {\ttfamily window\+\_\+title} can be arbitrary, to prevent nasty memory access. 
\end{DoxyWarning}

\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}

\begin{DoxyItemize}

\end{DoxyItemize}

\Hypertarget{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a652c48e6e55a89bc96c9ae24c99a828e}\index{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay@{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay}!wheel@{wheel}}
\index{wheel@{wheel}!cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay@{cimg\_library\_suffixed::CImgDisplay}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{wheel()}{wheel()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a652c48e6e55a89bc96c9ae24c99a828e} 
int cimg\+\_\+library\+\_\+suffixed\+::\+CImg\+Display\+::wheel (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



Return current state of the mouse wheel. 

\begin{DoxyNote}{Note}

\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item The returned value can be positive or negative depending on whether the mouse wheel has been scrolled forward or backward.
\item Scrolling the wheel forward add {\ttfamily 1} to the wheel value.
\item Scrolling the wheel backward substract {\ttfamily 1} to the wheel value.
\item The returned value cumulates the number of forward of backward scrolls since the creation of the display, or since the last reset of the wheel value (using set\+\_\+wheel()). It is strongly recommended to quickly reset the wheel counter when an action has been performed regarding the current wheel value. Otherwise, the returned wheel value may be for instance {\ttfamily 0} despite the fact that many scrolls have been done (as many in forward as in backward directions). 
\end{DoxyItemize}
\end{DoxyNote}
\begin{DoxyParagraph}{Example}

\begin{DoxyCode}{0}
\DoxyCodeLine{\mbox{\hyperlink{structcimg__library__suffixed_1_1CImgDisplay_a7716ca9a49ff94123b3417556bf4ce49}{CImgDisplay}}\ disp(400,400);}
\DoxyCodeLine{\textcolor{keywordflow}{while}\ (!disp.is\_closed())\ \{}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \textcolor{keywordflow}{if}\ (disp.wheel())\ \{}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ \textcolor{keywordtype}{int}\ counter\ =\ disp.wheel();\ \ \textcolor{comment}{//\ Read\ the\ state\ of\ the\ mouse\ wheel.}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ ...\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textcolor{comment}{//\ Do\ what\ you\ want\ with\ 'counter'.}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \ \ disp.set\_wheel();\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textcolor{comment}{//\ Reset\ the\ wheel\ value\ to\ 0.}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ \}}
\DoxyCodeLine{\ \ disp.wait();}
\DoxyCodeLine{\}}

\end{DoxyCode}
 
\end{DoxyParagraph}
